it's the only unblocked "high"-priority task, per the previous entry's own note. Created branch
`task/task-024-gateway-aggregations` from up-to-date `main` (confirmed clean, `git status`/`git log`
both worked at the very start of this session, before the shell tool died - see blocker below).

Explored the existing TASK-023 Gateway code first (Query.cs / GatewayReadContext.cs /
GatewayReadDbContext.cs / IGatewayReadContext.cs / entity+configuration classes / the
ProcessorSchemaSeed-based integration test harness / SchemaSnapshotTests' drift-check mechanism) via
a research subagent plus direct reads, to match its conventions exactly rather than inventing a
different shape. Confirmed via grep that no `FromSqlRaw`/`FromSqlInterpolated`/`date_trunc`/
`generate_series` usage exists anywhere in `src/` yet - this task is the first raw-SQL query in the
codebase, so there was no existing pattern to copy, only EF Core/Npgsql conventions to follow.

Design decisions (none of these are stated explicitly in PRD.md or tasks.json - documented here so
the next iteration doesn't have to re-derive them):
  - `aggregations(metricCode: String!, bucket: AggregationBucketSize!, from: DateTime!, to: DateTime!,
    location: String, meterType: String): [AggregationBucketReadModel!]!` - metricCode is mandatory
    (averaging/summing across different metrics, e.g. energy+co2, in one bucket makes no numeric
    sense), location/meterType are optional filters. Grouping is always by (location, meterType,
    bucketStart) - one row per meter-type-per-location-per-bucket, not per individual meter (matches
    the acceptance-criterion phrasing "grouped by location... returns exactly 24 buckets per
    location").
  - Empty-bucket semantics (genuinely unspecified in PRD F4/tasks.json, flagged explicitly by the
    research subagent as a gap): `count = 0` (non-nullable int) with `avg`/`min`/`max`/`sum` all
    `null` for a bucket with zero matching readings - chosen for consistency with how
    `ReadingReadModel.ValueNumeric`/`MeterCurrentValueReadModel` already represent "no value" as
    `decimal?` rather than `0` elsewhere in this codebase. If a reviewer wants `0` instead of `null`
    for avg/min/max/sum, that's a one-line change to the `AggregationBucketReadModel` mapping in
    `GatewayReadContext.GetAggregationsAsync`, not a redesign.
  - "Buckets with zero readings represented explicitly" is implemented as: `series` CTE = every
    distinct (location, meterType) pair from `meters` matching the location/meterType filters (NOT
    filtered by whether that meter ever reported `metricCode` - a meter that never reported the
    requested metric still gets a full run of zero-count buckets, which is the more literal reading
    of "explicitly represented" than silently omitting it); `buckets` CTE = `generate_series` over
    every bucket boundary in `[from, to)` using `date_trunc(unit, ...)` for alignment and
    `date_trunc(unit, to - interval '1 microsecond')` for the inclusive upper bound of
    `generate_series` (needed because `generate_series`'s end is inclusive, so without the
    microsecond subtraction a `to` that lands exactly on a bucket boundary would produce one bucket
    too many - e.g. a `[T, T+24h)` window would generate 25 hourly buckets instead of 24). `agg` CTE =
    the actual `GROUP BY m.location, m.meter_type, date_trunc(unit, r.observed_at)` over `readings`
    joined to `meters`. Final `SELECT` cross-joins `series x buckets` and `LEFT JOIN`s `agg`, so a
    bucket with no matching rows surfaces as `COALESCE(..., 0)`/`NULL` instead of being absent from
    the result set - all bucketing/grouping/zero-fill happens in one SQL statement (no client-side
    grouping after `.ToList()`), which is exactly what tasks.json's own test_steps step 2 asks to
    verify.
  - Implemented via `FromSqlInterpolated` (not `FromSqlRaw`) against a new keyless entity type
    `AggregationBucketRowEntity` (`Persistence/Entities/`, configured via
    `AggregationBucketRowEntityConfiguration` with `HasNoKey()` + `ToView(null)`, registered on
    `GatewayReadDbContext` the same explicit one-by-one way as every other entity in that context -
    the file's own comment already documents *why* it's not `ApplyConfigurationsFromAssembly`, see
    that comment before changing this). `date_trunc`'s unit argument and `generate_series`'s step are
    both passed as ordinary interpolated (therefore parameterized, not concatenated) string values -
    Postgres accepts `date_trunc($1, ...)` and `($1)::interval` with a bound text parameter, so this
    does not reopen any SQL-injection surface despite being the first raw-SQL query in the codebase.
    `IGatewayReadContext.GetAggregationsAsync` is a method, not an `IQueryable` property (matching the
    existing `GetCurrentValuesAsync` precedent), since HotChocolate's `[UseProjection]`/`[UseFiltering]`
    pipeline has nothing meaningful to compose onto a query whose shape (bucket count, grouping) is
    already fully determined by its own arguments.
  - `AggregationBucketSize` enum (`Minute`/`Hour`/`Day`) lives in
    `Gateway.Application/Readings/AggregationBucketSize.cs`, following the same
    `Application/<Feature>/<Enum>.cs` placement as `AlertSeverity`/`AlertOperator`/`AlertStatus` in
    `Application/Alerting/`. HotChocolate will render this as `enum AggregationBucketSize { MINUTE
    HOUR DAY }` in the schema (SCREAMING_SNAKE_CASE), matching how `AlertSeverity`/`AlertOperator`
    already render.

Files added:
  - `src/Gateway/WeakAppHandler.Gateway.Application/Readings/AggregationBucketSize.cs`
  - `src/Gateway/WeakAppHandler.Gateway.Application/Readings/AggregationBucketReadModel.cs`

  - `src/Gateway/WeakAppHandler.Gateway.Infrastructure/Persistence/Configurations/AggregationBucketRowEntityConfiguration.cs`

Picked up from an iteration whose shell tool died before it could build, test, or reach git even
once - everything above this paragraph is that iteration's own design record, preserved as-is.
Finishing it required real debugging, not just running the plan it left: the design itself was
sound, but the raw SQL had two genuine Postgres bugs neither `dotnet build` nor a code review would
catch, only `dotnet test` against a real database.

TWO REAL BUGS FOUND AND FIXED, both specific to `FromSqlInterpolated`'s parameter binding rather than
to anything about aggregation logic itself:
  1. `GROUP BY m.location, m.meter_type, date_trunc({unit}, r.observed_at)` failed with Postgres error
     42803 ("column ... must appear in the GROUP BY clause") even though the identical expression
     appeared in the SELECT list. Cause: `FromSqlInterpolated` binds every occurrence of an
     interpolated hole to its OWN parameter - so the SELECT list's `date_trunc({unit}, ...)` and the
     GROUP BY's textually-identical `date_trunc({unit}, ...)` compile to two DIFFERENT parameter
     placeholders bound to the same value at runtime, which Postgres's parser cannot prove equal
     during validation. Fixed by grouping on the SELECT list's own `bucket_start` alias instead of
     repeating the expression - Postgres allows GROUP BY to reference an output alias for exactly
     this reason.
  2. `({location} IS NULL OR m.location = {location})` failed with Postgres error 42P18 ("could not
     determine data type of parameter"). The `= {location}` branch gives its own parameter occurrence
     a type from context (matching `m.location`), but the bare `{location} IS NULL` branch's SEPARATE
     parameter occurrence has no adjacent expression to borrow a type from, and Postgres's
     prepared-statement protocol requires every parameter's type resolved before execution. Fixed by
     casting explicitly: `{location}::text IS NULL`. Applied to both the `location` and `meterType`
     null-checks, in both the `series` and `agg` CTEs (four occurrences total).
  Neither bug is specific to this codebase - both are well-known EF Core/Npgsql `FromSqlInterpolated`
  gotchas that only surface against a real Postgres server, which is exactly why TASK-014's Testcontainers
  harness exists rather than an in-memory provider standing in for one.

Also: renamed the interface's `to` parameter to `until` (kept `Query.GetAggregations`'s own public
GraphQL argument as `to`, which CA1716 does not flag on a non-virtual/non-interface member) - CA1716
rejected `to` as a parameter name on `IGatewayReadContext.GetAggregationsAsync` because it collides
with a reserved keyword in some .NET languages. `ProcessorSchemaSeed` gained two new seed helpers
(`SeedMeterAsync` for a bare meter, `AddReadingAsync` for one reading at an exact instant/value) since
the existing "N readings one minute apart from an anchor" helper could not place readings in specific
hour buckets. Metric codes in the new tests are fixed to the five real ones (`readings.metric_code`
has a foreign key onto Processor's `metrics` lookup table - it cannot take an arbitrary per-test code
the way Notification's alerting schema can); isolation between concurrently-running Facts instead
comes from each test's own unique location, always passed through the query's own `location` filter.

Testing - 3 new tests in `AggregationsQueryTests.cs`: a 24-hour window with hourly buckets returns
exactly 24 buckets including empty ones (count 0, avg/min/max/sum null) for hours with no readings
(acceptance criterion 1); three known values (10/20/30) in one bucket produce avg 20/min 10/max 30/
sum 60/count 3, matching hand arithmetic (acceptance criterion 3); a location filter excludes another
location's own (location, meterType) row from the result entirely, not merely from the assertions.
Test step 2 ("review the generated SQL to confirm no client-side grouping") is satisfied structurally
rather than by an assertion, documented on the test class: `GetAggregationsAsync` pipes
`FromSqlInterpolated` directly into `ToListAsync`, so there is no LINQ `GroupBy` step to review.

TASK-035: Frontend realtime client - SignalR AlertsHub + graphql-ws onReadingStored,
auto-reconnect with visible status, reconnect-then-refetch seam, strict channel separation

Picked as the highest-priority unblocked task (all pending tasks above it in priority - notably

Branch: task/task-035-realtime-client, from up-to-date main.

Confirmed before starting: no ApolloClient instance existed anywhere yet (TASK-033 only wired the
toolchain/codegen; App.tsx had no ApolloProvider) - so this task also had to build the GraphQL
client itself, not just its subscription transport, to have anywhere to plug graphql-ws into.
Read the Notification/Gateway Program.cs and the AlertsHub/SignalRAlertDispatcher source to get the
real wire shapes right: hub route is `/hubs/alerts`, dispatcher broadcasts via
`Clients.All.SendAsync("AlertRaised"/"AlertResolved", <AlertRaised|AlertResolved contract record>)`
with no cross-process backplane (single-replica only, matching the ADR already on file). Gateway's
Program.cs confirmed the WS transport rides the same `/graphql` endpoint HTTP does
(`app.UseWebSockets()` ahead of `MapGraphQL()`).

Installed `@microsoft/signalr` (resolved ^10.0.11) and `graphql-ws` (resolved ^6.2.1) - real
resolved versions from a real `npm install`, not guessed ranges.

What was built (all under `frontend/src/shared/`):
  - `config/runtimeConfig.ts` + `.env.example` - the two backend URLs (Gateway GraphQL HTTP+WS,
    Notification's alerts hub), defaulting to the `https` `dotnet run` launchSettings profiles
    (7069/7031) rather than docker-compose, since TASK-047 hasn't put either service in the
    compose stack yet. Override via `.env.local` (gitignored by Vite's own `*.local` rule already
    in place).
  - `auth/accessTokenStore.ts` - a deliberately tiny module-level `getAccessToken`/`setAccessToken`
    seam (defaults to `null`) shared by both transports below, so TASK-041's login flow has one
    place to plug a real token into instead of touching either transport again. Until then every
    backend service falls back to its own `Auth:DevBypassEnabled` dev-bypass (confirmed by reading
    ServiceAuthenticationExtensions.cs - defaults to `false`, so a service actually needs
    `Auth:DevBypassEnabled=true` set locally, e.g. via `ASPNETCORE_Auth__DevBypassEnabled` env var,
    to accept unauthenticated dev traffic; not committed anywhere, so whoever runs the backend
    services locally for manual QA of this feature needs to set that themselves until TASK-041/042
    land).
  - `realtime/connectionStatus.ts` - the `ConnectionStatus` union
    (`connecting|connected|reconnecting|disconnected`) plus a pure `combineConnectionStatus`
    (worse-of-two) used to fold both channels into one indicator.
  - `realtime/alertsHubClient.ts` - one module-singleton `HubConnection` to `AlertsHub` (built, not
    started, at import time - starting is `ensureAlertsHubStarted()`'s job, called from the hook
    below so React's effect lifecycle - including StrictMode's deliberate double-invoke - drives
    it idempotently rather than a raw module side effect). `withAutomaticReconnect()` (default
    policy: PRD asks for reconnect-then-refetch behaviour, not a bespoke backoff schedule).
    Exposes `onAlertsHubStatusChange` and, critically, `onAlertsHubReconnected` - fires ONLY from
    the real `onreconnected` event, never from the first successful connect, which is the exact
    distinction TASK-039's future alerts list needs to know when to refetch.
  - `realtime/useAlertsHub.ts` - the ONLY place in the app allowed to call
    `alertsHubConnection.on('AlertRaised'|'AlertResolved', ...)`; callers pass
    `onAlertRaised`/`onAlertResolved`/`onReconnected` as options instead of reaching into the
    connection directly - structurally rules out the same alert event ever being wired up twice.
  - `realtime/apolloClient.ts` - `ApolloClient` with `split()` routing subscription operations to a
    `GraphQLWsLink` (graphql-ws `createClient`) and everything else to `HttpLink`, with a
    `SetContextLink` reading `getAccessToken()` fresh on every request (not once at module load) so
    a token set later by TASK-041 is picked up immediately. `lazy: false` - connects at app startup
    rather than waiting for the first `onReadingStored` subscriber, since the status indicator
    needs to reflect this channel's real state as soon as the app loads. Status derivation needed
    no bespoke bookkeeping: graphql-ws's own `on.connecting(isRetry)`/`on.connected(wasRetry)`
    callbacks already say whether a transition is a first connect or a retry.
  - `realtime/useOnReadingStoredSubscription.ts` - the ONLY place allowed to
    `useSubscription(ON_READING_STORED, ...)`; wraps Apollo's generated `graphql()`-tagged typed
    document (added to codegen's input, regenerated `src/gql/`) with a small
    `{ location?, meterType?, skip? }` options surface for TASK-036/038's future consumers. No
    reconnect-refetch needed here unlike alerts - there's no persisted "readings list" query this
    stream backs; graphql-ws itself resumes the live subscription automatically after a WS
    reconnect, no hand-rolled catch-up logic required.
  - `realtime/useRealtimeConnectionStatus.ts` + `realtime/ConnectionStatusBadge.tsx` (+ css) - folds
    both channels via `combineConnectionStatus` into one `role="status"` badge
    (Connecting.../Live/Reconnecting.../Offline), now rendered in `AppShell`'s header. Deliberately
    plain neutral colors (`--color-text-muted`/`--color-accent`), NOT the severity scale - TASK-034
    reserved `--color-severity-*` exclusively for meter/alert severity, and connection health isn't
    that vocabulary.
  - `app/App.tsx` now wraps everything in `<ApolloProvider client={apolloClient}>` (outermost,
    since nothing below depends on Apollo either, but Apollo has no dependency on router/announcer).

Picked up from an iteration whose shell tool died before it could build, test, or reach git even
once (the same recurring environment failure this file's TASK-020/024/029/031/032 entries document).
The scaffold and package choices above are that iteration's own record, preserved as-is; verifying
it required actual debugging - three real, independent problems surfaced only once `npm run build`/
`npm run lint` actually ran, none visible from reading the config files alone:

  1. **Codegen output failed tsc outright.** The `client-preset`'s default output uses real
     `import { X }` for type-only imports (rejected by this scaffold's own `verbatimModuleSyntax`)
     and a real `enum` declaration (rejected by `erasableSyntaxOnly`, a TypeScript 6 flag that
     forbids syntax the compiler cannot erase without emitting runtime code). Fixed by passing
     `config: { useTypeImports: true, enumsAsTypes: true }` to the `client-preset` generate entry in
     `codegen.ts` - both are the intended knobs for exactly this mismatch, not a workaround.
  2. **`noUnusedLocals` failed on the generated output's own top-level import**, because
     `ignoreNoDocuments: true` means an empty scaffold's codegen run produces a `gql.ts` whose
     `import * as types from './graphql'` has nothing yet to reference. Fixed by excluding
     `src/gql` from `tsconfig.app.json`'s own root file set (`"exclude": ["src/gql"]`) - the same
     "generated, never hand-checked in isolation" treatment `eslint.config.js` already gives it.
     Once real code imports from `src/gql`, that import becomes used and it is type-checked
     normally as a dependency of the file that imports it.
  3. **`eslint-plugin-react-hooks@7.1.1`'s own `configs['recommended-latest']` is not flat-config
     shaped** - its `plugins` field is a legacy string array (`["react-hooks"]`), which ESLint 10
     rejects outright rather than auto-converting ("Oops! ... plugins key defined as an array of
     strings"). The correctly-shaped equivalent lives one level deeper, at
     `configs.flat['recommended-latest']` (an object keyed by plugin name) - confirmed by loading
     the installed package directly in a `node -e` probe rather than guessing from documentation,
     since this is exactly the kind of cross-major-version API drift that has bitten
     HotChocolate-facing work earlier in this backlog (TASK-025/031/032's entries).
  None of these three are specific to this codebase's conventions - all three are current, genuine
  incompatibilities between the newest `create-vite react-ts` template's strictness defaults and
  the graphql-codegen/eslint-plugin-react-hooks ecosystem as it exists right now.

Also decided (not previously specified): `src/gql` is gitignored rather than committed, matching
`dist/`'s own treatment as a build artifact - it is fully deterministic from the committed
`schema.graphql` plus whatever documents exist, so committing it would only invite drift/merge
noise for no benefit. `package.json` gained a `"prebuild": "npm run codegen"` script so `npm run
build` alone still regenerates it first (verified: a fresh `npm install` with no `src/gql` present
at all still builds clean via this hook). `README.md` was rewritten - the original was the
generic Vite/oxlint boilerplate, already inapplicable to this project (oxlint was swapped for
ESLint) and pointed at a `.oxlintrc.json` this task's own scaffolding history had already removed
the working config for. The three genuinely stale files this session's predecessor could not delete

  - Color semantics (`src/index.css`): one `--color-accent` (links/primary actions/focus rings/
    active nav) plus a three-step `--color-severity-{normal,warning,critical}` scale with matching
    `-bg` tints, documented in comments as reserved exclusively for meter/alert severity - nothing

    039 to reuse instead of re-inventing severity styling per-screen.
  - `src/shared/ui/async-boundary/AsyncBoundary.tsx` - the task's centerpiece. Generic
    `AsyncBoundary<T>` with `loading`/`error`/`data`/`isEmpty`/`onRetry`/`skeleton`/`emptyState`/
    `renderError`/`children` props. Derives exactly four render paths (error-with-no-data ->
    default or custom retry panel with `role="alert"`; loading-with-no-data -> caller's skeleton,
    never a spinner overlay; no-usable-data -> empty state; otherwise -> `children(data, {
    isStale })`), where `isStale = Boolean(error) || loading` covers BOTH acceptance criteria at
    once: a background refetch (`loading` true, data already present) shows a "Refreshing…"
    `role="status"` banner without discarding the visible data, and a refetch that errors
    (`error` true, data already present) shows a "Showing last known data..." banner with its own
    Retry button instead of the screen going blank - the specific behaviour TASK-034's acceptance
    criteria calls out by name. One real type bug caught and fixed while writing this: deriving a
    single `hasData` boolean and branching on `!hasData` does NOT let TypeScript narrow `data` from
    `T | undefined` to `T` before calling `children(data, ...)` - control-flow narrowing tracks the
    literal comparison, not a boolean proxy for it. Fixed by re-checking the literal
    `data === undefined` in the last guard clause (`if (!hasData || data === undefined) return
    ...`), which gives real narrowing for free below it - documented inline since it's a genuine
    "the boolean summary lied to the type checker" gotcha, not stylistic.
  - `src/shared/ui/skeleton/Skeleton.tsx` - a small reusable shimmering-bars placeholder so
    `AsyncBoundary` callers (TASK-036 onward) don't each hand-roll a skeleton; respects
    `prefers-reduced-motion`.
  - `src/shared/a11y/` - the ARIA live region primitive, split into THREE files
    (`announcer-context.ts` for the bare `createContext` + type, `AnnouncerProvider.tsx` for the
    provider component, `useAnnouncer.ts` for the hook) rather than one combined file. This was a
    deliberate defensive call, not the original design: `eslint-plugin-react-refresh`'s
    `only-export-components` rule (already wired into `eslint.config.js` via
    `reactRefresh.configs.vite`) is known to flag a file that exports both a component and a hook
    together, and since the shell died before `npm run lint` could actually confirm this either
    way, splitting into component-only / hook-only / no-component files sidesteps the question
    entirely rather than shipping something that might fail lint unverified. One shared
    `aria-live="polite" aria-atomic="true"` region is rendered once by `AnnouncerProvider`; a
    zero-width-space parity trick forces a DOM text change (and thus an actual SR announcement)
    even for two consecutive identical messages. Wired to a real, non-decorative use immediately:
    `app/layout/RouteAnnouncer.tsx` announces "Navigated to <page>" on every client-side route
    change (skipping the very first render), which is a real SPA accessibility requirement (screen
    readers get no automatic page-changed announcement without a full browser navigation) and also
    proves the primitive end-to-end for TASK-039/035 to reuse for alert-raised/resolved events
    later.
  - `src/app/layout/AppShell.tsx` (+`app-shell.css`, `nav-items.ts`) - header with brand + a
    `<nav aria-label="Main">` of four `NavLink`s (Overview/History/Alerts/Administration, active
    link gets `aria-current="page"` automatically from react-router plus an accent underline), a
    `.app-shell__skip-link` (visually-hidden until focused, jumps to `#main-content`), and
    `<main id="main-content" tabIndex={-1}>` wrapping `<Outlet />` - the standard skip-link pattern
    (href target must be focusable via `tabIndex={-1}` even though it's not itself in the natural
    tab order). `src/app/App.tsx` now wires `AnnouncerProvider` > `BrowserRouter` > `Routes` with
    four placeholder pages under `src/app/pages/` (`OverviewPage`/`HistoryPage`/`AlertsPage`/
    `AdministrationPage`, each just a heading + a one-line pointer to the task that will build the
    real screen - TASK-036/038/039/040 respectively) so routing/layout has something real to
    render without pre-building screens that are explicitly later tasks' own scope.

    tracked, not forgotten.
  - Tests written (NOT YET RUN - see BLOCKER): `AsyncBoundary.test.tsx` (6 cases: skeleton-only
    while loading with no data; empty state when `isEmpty` says so; error state with a working
    Retry button via `@testing-library/user-event`, asserting `role="alert"`; loaded-with-no-
    staleness-banner when everything is fresh; error-with-existing-data keeps data on screen behind
    a `role="status"` banner; loading-with-existing-data shows "Refreshing…" instead of the
    skeleton). `AppShell.test.tsx` (4 cases: skip link href + focusable main target; all four nav
    links present inside the `nav` landmark; Tab alone reaches the skip link then all four nav
    links in DOM order, one `toHaveFocus()` per stop; activating a focused nav link with Enter
    navigates and updates `aria-current`). `announcer.test.tsx` (2 cases: the live region starts
    empty with the right `aria-live`/`aria-atomic` attributes; `announce()` from `useAnnouncer`
    lands in the region's text content). These cover TASK-034's acceptance criteria about as far as
    RTL/jsdom can reach - the criterion about focus being "visually" visible is a CSS/rendering
    claim RTL cannot assert; the `:focus-visible` outline rule in `index.css` is the corresponding
    implementation, unverified by an actual browser screenshot this session (no browser-driving
    tool was available/attempted here beyond what RTL's jsdom gives).

Picked up from an iteration whose shell tool died mid-session (the same recurring environment
failure this file's TASK-020/024/029/031/032/033 entries document) right after its two `npm
install` calls completed but before it could build, lint, test, or reach git even once. The design
above is that iteration's own record, preserved as-is - verifying it surfaced two real, independent
bugs, both exactly the kind "reasoning by inspection" cannot catch:

  1. **A literal invisible character in source.** `AnnouncerProvider`'s zero-width-space parity
     trick was written as an actual U+200B byte sitting unescaped in the `.tsx` file
     (``${next}<ZWSP>` `` in a template literal), which `npm run lint` correctly rejected via
     `no-irregular-whitespace` - an invisible byte in source is a real hazard on its own, not just a
     lint technicality. Fixed by building the character from its code point instead
     (`String.fromCharCode(0x200b)`), the byte-for-byte identical runtime string with nothing
     invisible left in the file. (Worth a general note for future edits: getting an AI assistant to
     literally type an escape sequence like `​` into a file is unreliable - the model tends to
     render the actual character it names rather than the six ASCII bytes of the escape syntax, so
     `String.fromCharCode`/`fromCodePoint` is the dependable way to introduce a specific code point
     into source without that ambiguity.)
  2. **Every AsyncBoundary test after the first two failed with "found multiple elements."** This
     project's `vite.config.ts` test block has no `test.globals: true`, and every test file
     explicitly imports `describe`/`it`/`afterEach`/etc. from `'vitest'` rather than relying on
     injected globals - but `@testing-library/react`'s own automatic `afterEach(cleanup)` only
     registers itself when it finds a GLOBAL `afterEach` on `globalThis` at import time, which
     requires exactly the `globals: true` this project deliberately does not set. That detection
     silently no-ops, so every `render()` call left its tree in jsdom's one shared `document`
     without ever being unmounted, and later tests in the same file saw earlier tests' leftover DOM
     alongside their own. Fixed with one line in the shared `setup-tests.ts`:
     `afterEach(cleanup)` (explicit imports of both from `'vitest'`/`'@testing-library/react'`) -
     registered once, in the one place `setupFiles` exists for, rather than requiring every test
     file to remember it or changing this project's explicit-import testing convention to use
     globals just to make RTL's own auto-detection work.
  Neither bug is specific to this codebase's conventions - the second in particular is a well-known
  gotcha for any Vitest + Testing Library project that opts out of `test.globals`.

Also: deleted the two orphaned empty files the predecessor's tooling could not remove that session
(`frontend/src/app/App.css`, superseded by `AppShell`'s own CSS; `frontend/src/shared/a11y/
announcer.tsx`, superseded by the three-file `AnnouncerProvider`/`useAnnouncer`/context split) -
confirmed nothing imports either before removing them.

VERIFIED, three times from progressively cleaner states (incremental re-run; `rm -rf src/gql dist`;
a full `rm -rf node_modules src/gql dist` + fresh `npm install`), matching the discipline TASK-033's
own entry established for this same package: `npm run build` (via `prebuild`'s codegen regeneration)
-> 35 modules transformed, 0 TypeScript errors. `npm run lint` -> clean. `npm test` -> 3 test files,
12/12 passed (`AsyncBoundary.test.tsx` x6, `AppShell.test.tsx` x4, `announcer.test.tsx` x2). The

cannot assert by itself - the `:focus-visible` outline rule in `index.css` is the implementation,
unverified by an actual rendered screenshot (no browser-driving tool was used this session either).
  - tasks.json: TASK-034 status -> "done".

Picked as the highest-priority genuinely-unblocked task this session: of the pending tasks whose

Confirmed before starting: the Gateway schema (`src/Gateway/WeakAppHandler.Gateway.Api/schema.graphql`)
already exposes everything needed - `meters { id location meterType lastSeenAt currentValues {
metricCode valueNumeric valueBool observedAt } }` and `alerts(where: { status: { eq: ACTIVE } })
{ nodes { id meterId severity } }` - so this task is 100% frontend, no backend/schema changes.
Also confirmed a real gap: `MeterReadModel`/`MeterCurrentValueReadModel` expose no unit/display-name
for a metric even though Processor's `metrics` seed table has one (`MetricSeedData.cs`) - hardcoded
a small client-side lookup (`entities/meter/metricDisplay.ts`) from that same seed data
(energy->kWh, co2->ppm, pm25->µg/m³, humidity->%, motion_detected->boolean) rather than blocking on
a backend change; flagged as a known gap for whoever next touches the Gateway schema, not silently
worked around.

What was built:
  - `entities/meter/metricDisplay.ts` (+test) - `getMetricDisplayInfo`/`formatMetricValue`, the
    client-side metric metadata lookup above. Numeric formatting and `shared/time/relativeTime.ts`'s
    `Intl.RelativeTimeFormat` are both pinned to the `'en-US'` locale explicitly rather than
    `undefined` (the runtime default) - this machine's default locale is `ru-RU`, which silently
    flipped every formatted number/relative-time string in tests and would have done the same in
    any deployment with a non-English OS/browser locale, while every other label in the UI
    (`AppShell` nav, `AsyncBoundary`, this screen's own headings) is English-only. Caught by actually
    running the test suite, not by reading the code - `4.2 kWh` rendered as `4,2 kWh` and every
    relative-time string came back in Russian on the first `npm test` run.
  - `shared/time/relativeTime.ts` (+test) + `shared/time/useNow.ts` - pure `formatRelativeTime(iso,
    now)` (`Intl.RelativeTimeFormat`, walks year/month/day/hour/minute/second) and a `useNow(
    intervalMs = 30_000)` hook that re-renders on an interval so "N minutes ago" labels don't go
    stale while a tile sits on screen without a data refresh.
  - `features/overview/overviewSeverity.ts` (+test) - `buildMeterSeverityMap(alerts)`, a pure
    function picking the worst active alert per meter and mapping it to a tile severity/label pair.
    Backend `AlertSeverity` has three tiers (Info/Warning/Critical) but the UI's `Severity` type
    (`shared/ui/severity/Severity.ts`, from TASK-034) only has two non-normal tiers
    (warning/critical) - INFO alerts (the seeded "Humidity above 70%" rule) share the `warning` tile
    color with WARNING alerts but keep a distinct `"Info"` text label, since color alone must never
    be how severity is conveyed (same rule `SeverityIndicator` already followed) and there's no
    fourth tile color to introduce cleanly.
  - `features/overview/groupMetersByLocation.ts` (+test) - pure grouping helper, preserves each
    location's first-seen order from the (already server-sorted) meter list.
  - `features/overview/useOverviewData.ts` - the `OverviewData` GraphQL query (`meters` +
    active `alerts`, added to codegen's inline-document input, regenerated `src/gql/`) plus the
    realtime wiring: any `onReadingStored` event or `AlertRaised`/`AlertResolved` event schedules a
    500ms-debounced `refetch()` (coalesces a whole poll cycle's worth of per-meter events - up to 18
    - into one network round-trip instead of one refetch per event), while `useAlertsHub`'s
    `onReconnected` triggers an immediate, non-debounced refetch to reconcile whatever was missed
    while disconnected (the exact seam TASK-035 built and left unconsumed for this task). Server
    data stays the single source of truth throughout - no client-side merging of realtime payloads
    into the Apollo cache, deliberately, to avoid a second, divergent copy of severity-mapping logic.
  - `features/overview/{OverviewHeader,MeterTile,LocationSection,OverviewContent}.tsx` (+css) - the
    presentational layer. `OverviewContent` is the one piece of real UI logic that is NOT
    query-aware (takes an already-resolved data shape + `now` as props) specifically so it could be
    unit-tested with plain RTL, no `MockedProvider`/subscription mocking involved - `OverviewPage`
    itself (the thin `useOverviewData` + `AsyncBoundary` + `OverviewContent` wrapper) is intentionally
    NOT unit-tested for the same reason `ConnectionStatusBadge`'s full realtime chain needed the
    global signalr/graphql-ws mocks in TASK-035: exercising it for real would mean either fighting
    `MockedProvider`'s exact-variables matching for a subscription document mixed with a query, or
    hand-rolling cache doubles, neither of which was worth it for a route wrapper with almost no
    logic of its own.
  - `shared/ui/severity/SeverityIndicator.tsx` - updated its own doc comment: it is no longer the
    *only* consumer of `--color-severity-*`. `MeterTile`'s own background/left-border (via a
    `data-severity` attribute) is a second, deliberate consumer, because TASK-036's acceptance
    criterion is explicit that the *tile* itself (not just an indicator badge inside it) must change
    color - documented the second consumer explicitly rather than silently breaking the "only place"
    comment's claim.
  - `app/pages/OverviewPage.tsx` - rewritten from TASK-034's placeholder to the real screen
    (`useOverviewData` + `useNow` + `AsyncBoundary` wrapping `OverviewContent`).

NOT done in this session (explicitly out of scope, not an oversight):
  - No manual real-browser verification against a running Gateway/Notification/Processor stack

    Gateway and Notification (neither is in docker-compose yet, TASK-047) plus Processor actually
    ingesting data. `OverviewContent`'s RTL tests cover the acceptance criteria's actual claims
    (value/unit/relative-time per tile, severity-driven tile color, header counts matching the

  - "Reporting meters" in the header is simply `meters.length` (every meter the Gateway returns);
    "last successful poll" is `max(meters.lastSeenAt)` rather than a dedicated ingestion-status call
    - Gateway has no REST proxy to Ingestor's `/status` yet (that's TASK-026, still pending and not
    a dependency of this task), and `lastSeenAt` is already updated by Processor on every successful
    poll containing that meter (TASK-019), so it's a correct, already-available signal rather than a
    placeholder.

    server-side total (`AlertsConnection` has no `totalCount` field in the current schema). Fine at
    this project's scale (5 seed rules, 18 meters) but would undercount past 100 simultaneously
    active alerts - noted for whoever adds `totalCount` to the Gateway schema or wires up TASK-039's
    own alerts list, which will hit the same ceiling.

[2026-09-03] TASK-039 - DONE

================================================================================

Picked as the highest-priority genuinely-unblocked task this session: of the pending tasks whose
dependencies were all `done` (TASK-007/021/030/037 at "medium", TASK-039 at "high" - the rest
gated behind TASK-021/026/030/037/041 which are still pending), TASK-039 was the sole "high"
candidate, matching TASK-036's own NEXT note calling it out as "the other tied-highest candidate".
Branch `task/task-039-alerts-screen` from up-to-date main. Confirmed before starting: the Gateway
schema (`schema.graphql`) already exposes everything needed on `AlertReadModel` (location,
meterType, metricCode, status, severity, triggeredAt/triggeredValueNumeric/triggeredValueBool,
resolvedAt/resolvedValueNumeric/resolvedValueBool) - no backend/schema change required, this task
is 100% frontend.

What was built:
  - `entities/alert/alertSeverityDisplay.ts` (+test) - `toDisplaySeverity`/`compareAlertSeverity`,
    extracted out of TASK-036's `features/overview/overviewSeverity.ts` (which had this exact
    INFO/WARNING/CRITICAL -> tile-severity mapping inlined and unexported). Alerts screen needed
    the identical mapping for each row's `SeverityIndicator`, so rather than re-inlining a second
    copy of the same switch statement, promoted it to a shared `entities/alert` module and had
    `overviewSeverity.ts` import from it - `buildMeterSeverityMap`'s own behavior/exports/tests are
    unchanged, this is a pure extraction with no behavior change (verified by `overviewSeverity.
    test.ts` still passing unmodified).
  - `features/alerts/alertFilters.ts` (+test) - `filterAlerts(alerts, { status, severity })`, pure
    client-side narrowing (`'ALL'` or an exact `AlertStatus`/`AlertSeverity` match on each). Kept
    client-side rather than pushing `status`/`severity` into the GraphQL `where` clause: the whole
    feed is one `first: 100` fetch already (see gap note below), and filtering client-side means
    changing a filter doesn't trigger a network round-trip.
  - `features/alerts/alertDuration.ts` (+test) - `formatAlertDuration(triggeredAt, resolvedAt)`,
    pure duration formatter for a resolved alert's triggered->resolved span: "45s" under a minute,
    "5m 32s" under an hour, "1h 05m" under a day, "2d 03h" beyond that - always the two most
    significant units so a multi-day alert isn't buried in a `HH:MM:SS`-style readout.
  - `features/alerts/{AlertsFilterBar,AlertRow,AlertsContent}.tsx` (+css, +AlertsContent.test.tsx)
    - the presentational layer. `AlertsContent` is the pure, query-unaware piece (data + now +
    filters + highlightedIds as props, same split OverviewContent established) so it could be unit-
    tested with plain RTL - covers server-order rendering, status/severity filtering individually
    and combined, the empty-filtered-list message, the resolved-duration line, the `data-new`
    highlight attribute, and the filter `<select>` wiring via `@testing-library/user-event`.
    `AlertRow` reuses `MeterTile`'s established pattern of a `data-severity` attribute for CSS
    (INFO/WARNING both render the "warning" tile color via `alertSeverityDisplay`, distinguished
    only by the `SeverityIndicator`'s text label, never by color alone) plus a `data-new` attribute
    that drives a 3s CSS highlight animation (`alert-row.css`) for a row just surfaced by a realtime
    event - a plain visual call-out, not a toast/banner, so it doesn't fight the existing
    `AsyncBoundary` staleness banner for attention.
  - `features/alerts/useAlertsData.ts` - the `AlertsData` query (`alerts(order: [{ triggeredAt:
    DESC }], first: 100) { nodes { ... } }`) wired to two realtime concerns: (1) the same
    debounce-then-refetch shape `useOverviewData` established (an `AlertRaised`/`AlertResolved`
    event schedules a 500ms-debounced `refetch()`; an AlertsHub reconnect triggers an immediate one,
    reusing the exact reconnect-then-refetch seam TASK-035 built), and (2) two things specific to
    this screen - a short-lived `highlightedIds` set (`Set<string>` of alert IDs, added the instant
    the hub event arrives and auto-removed after 3s, independent of whether the debounced refetch
    has actually landed yet) so `AlertRow` can render its `data-new` highlight, and a screen-reader
    announcement per event ("New critical alert in Garage: co2" / "Resolved warning alert in
    Kitchen: humidity") via the app's shared `AnnouncerProvider` (`useAnnouncer`) - the exact
    consumer TASK-034's own doc comment and TASK-036's entry both flagged this primitive as still
    waiting for. New alerts land at the top of the feed "for free" because the server already
    orders by `triggeredAt DESC` and a freshly-triggered alert's `triggeredAt` is the newest value
    in the set - no separate client-side reordering was needed to satisfy that acceptance criterion.
  - `app/pages/AlertsPage.tsx` - rewritten from TASK-034's placeholder: `useAlertsData` + `useNow` +
    local `filters` state (`useState<AlertFilters>`, owned by the page since `AlertsContent` is
    pure) + `AsyncBoundary` (empty-state check is the *unfiltered* count, so "no alerts have ever
    fired" and "current filter combination matches nothing" are two distinct, correctly different
    messages - the former is `AsyncBoundary`'s empty state, the latter is `AlertsContent`'s own
    "No alerts match the current filters." line).

Known gap carried forward (not fixed here, flagged for whoever touches it next): `alerts` is fetched
as a single `first: 100` page with no pagination UI, same ceiling TASK-036's own entry already noted
for the Overview header's alert count - fine at this project's seed scale (5 rules, 18 meters) but
would silently truncate past 100 total alerts (active+resolved combined, since this screen fetches
all statuses unlike Overview's active-only query). Whoever adds real pagination or a `totalCount`
field to the Gateway schema should revisit both call sites together.

    `Processor.Infrastructure/Ingestion/`) - `IConsumer<Fault<ReadingsIngested>>` /
    `IConsumer<Fault<IngestAttemptRecorded>>`. MassTransit publishes `Fault<T>` by default once a
    receive endpoint's retry policy is exhausted; these are registered via plain `bus.AddConsumer<T>`
    (not on an explicit receive endpoint like the consumers they shadow) so `ConfigureEndpoints` gives
    each its own convention-named queue, since a dead-lettered message and the fault event describing
    it are two independent deliveries.
  - `ProcessorInfrastructureServiceCollectionExtensions` - registers `ProcessingStatsState` as a
    `TryAddSingleton`.
  - `Processor.Worker/Admin/{ProcessingAdminController,ProcessingStatsResponse}.cs` - new

    exactly. Guarded by `ServicePolicies.IngestionAdmin` reused as-is rather than a new
    `ProcessingAdminPolicy`/`processing:admin` scope: the PRD's task text only asks for "a machine
    JWT" (no distinct scope like TASK-017's), the one seeded service client
    (`AuthSeedData.ServiceClientId = "gateway-ingestor"`) already carries `ingestion:admin` and is the
    Gateway's own client for both admin surfaces (TASK-026 proxies both to the same Gateway), and
    `Scopes` is an EF `PrimitiveCollection<string[]>` baked into `HasData` migrations - granting a
    second scope to the seeded client would need a new Auth Service data migration, which is scope
    creep for a task that doesn't ask for scope separation. Updated `ServicePolicies.IngestionAdmin`'s
    doc comment to say it now guards both surfaces, with a note that a dedicated scope can be split
    out later if the two ever need different grantees (e.g. TASK-043's least-privilege pass).

RECOVERY (this session): a prior session hit a total PowerShell tool death right after fixing a
StyleCop SA1201 ordering issue in `TestMeters.cs`, before a single `dotnet build`/`dotnet test` could
run against the new project - leaving it uncommitted but otherwise complete. This session found that
state, reviewed every new/changed file by hand (all sound), and ran the real verification:

  - `dotnet build m2.slnf` - 0 warnings, 0 errors.
  - `dotnet test WeakAppHandler.M2.IntegrationTests` - 5 of 6 failed with
    `System.TimeoutException: Message ... was consumed 0 times within 00:00:30, expected 1`.

Root-caused rather than reported: all three call sites in `EndToEndScenarioTests.cs` started the
`IngestorEndToEndHost` BEFORE the `ProcessorEndToEndHost`. The Ingestor's polling worker fires its
first poll immediately on host startup (by design), but a RabbitMQ topic exchange drops a message for
any binding that does not exist yet - so with the Processor started second, its queues were not yet
bound when that first poll published, and the message was silently lost. Fixed by swapping the start
order at all three sites (Processor first, then Ingestor) with a comment explaining why. Re-ran after
the fix: 6/6 passed. Also re-ran the full `WeakAppHandler.sln` suite (211 tests across all projects)
to check for regressions from adding the new project to the solution - all passed, 0 warnings on a
full rebuild.

Separately noted, not fixed (out of scope for this task): neither this new `IngestorEndToEndHost` nor
the pre-existing `WeakAppHandler.Ingestor.Tests.IngestorHost` (an established pattern from an earlier
task) ever calls `AddWeakAppClient()`, which is what binds `WeakAppOptions` from configuration - so
`WeakApp:PollingIntervalSeconds`'s in-memory override in both hosts has no effect, and the loop always
runs on the class's own hardcoded default (10s) regardless of what the test sets. Harmless today since
every assertion in both suites completes in a few seconds, well inside that 10s window, but worth a
follow-up if a slower CI box ever makes these suites flaky.

    into separate segments across a gap (a bucket with no reading) rather than interpolating through
    it. Falls back to the average value itself when min/max are absent but avg exists.

    1 and 24px so a handful of buckets do not render enormous bars.

    `max` or `bucketStart`/`count`) - no GraphQL query of their own, which is correctly TASK-038's job
    (wiring them into the History screen against the Gateway's real aggregations query, TASK-024).

    `MotionBarChart` for `count`, `RangeLineChart` (+ `EnergySummary` above it for `sum-avg`)

  - `HistoryPage.tsx` - wires `HistoryFilterBar`/`AsyncBoundary`/`Skeleton` (TASK-034's established

[2026-09-04] TASK-026 - DONE
Gateway REST: proxies ingestion/processing status (machine JWT to Ingestor/Processor), streams a
CSV export of readings with constant memory, and exposes an OpenAPI spec browsable through Swagger
UI in Development.
================================================================================

Picked per TASK-038's own NEXT note (tied with TASK-030 for unblocking TASK-040; picked first as the
lower-numbered of the two). Branch `task/task-026-gateway-rest-proxy-export` from up-to-date main.

Found the branch as an empty checkout - a ralph.sh iteration had created it and then failed
immediately (exit 1, zero commits, zero diff from main) before writing any code, so this was a full
implementation from scratch rather than a review-and-fix.

What was built, after researching the actual acceptance criteria against real code rather than
guessing: this is the FIRST case of one service in this codebase calling another over real HTTP as a
machine client (every prior admin-API test minted its own token; no production code path existed).
  - `Processor.Worker/Properties/launchSettings.json` (new) - Processor.Worker never had a fixed dev
    port (TASK-021 added its admin API but no way to actually reach it outside a test's in-memory
    TestServer). Assigned 5097, the next free port after Ingestor's 5095/Auth's 5091/Notification's
    5299/Gateway's 5173, mirroring Ingestor's own launchSettings.json shape exactly.
  - `Gateway.Api/appsettings.json` - new `ServiceClient` (TokenUri/ClientId/ClientSecret - reuses the
    seeded `gateway-ingestor` client TASK-017/021 already granted `ingestion:admin`, not a second
    client: one scope, no second Auth Service data migration, the same reasoning TASK-021's own
    design note gives) and `Services` (Ingestor/Processor base URLs) sections.
  - `Gateway.Api/ServiceClients/{ServiceClientOptions,ServiceClientTokenProvider,DownstreamServiceNames,
    ServiceClientsServiceCollectionExtensions}.cs` (new) - mints and caches the machine token
    (`System.Threading.Lock` guarding the synchronous cache read/write, a `SemaphoreSlim` serialising
    the actual mint call so concurrent cache misses coalesce onto one `/token` request rather than
    each minting their own - the same combination `IngestionRuntimeState` already established for
    sync state, extended for an async mint step it never needed), refreshed 30s ahead of the token's
    own expiry.
  - `Gateway.Api/Admin/AdminProxyController.cs` (new) - `GET /api/v1/ingestion/status` and
    `GET /api/v1/processing/stats`, each forwarding the downstream response verbatim (body, content
    type, status code) rather than deserialising into a Gateway-side DTO - what makes "the proxied
    response is the same data as calling the service directly" true by construction instead of by
    keeping two copies of the same shape in sync.
  - `Gateway.Api/Export/ReadingsExportController.cs` (new) - `GET /api/v1/readings/export` streams
    CSV via `IGatewayReadContext.Readings.AsAsyncEnumerable()` (an EF Core `IQueryable` over a
    `NoTracking` context - confirmed via `GatewayReadDbContext`'s own `UseQueryTrackingBehavior`) row
    by row directly onto `Response.Body`, never `ToListAsync`, which is what keeps memory constant
    regardless of range size. Optional from/to/location/meterType/metricCode filters translate into
    the same SQL WHERE clause any other query against `Readings` would produce.
  - Swagger UI: added `Swashbuckle.AspNetCore.SwaggerUI` (UI package only, no SwaggerGen - it just
    views the JSON document `Microsoft.AspNetCore.OpenApi`'s existing `MapOpenApi()` already
    produces), wired via `UseSwaggerUI` inside the same `IsDevelopment()` gate `MapOpenApi()` already
    uses.
  - Left the new REST routes without `[Authorize]`: the Gateway's own inbound JWT validation isn't
    configured yet either (no `Auth` section in its appsettings.json at all, `DevBypassEnabled`
    unset) - that is TASK-041/042's job per `ServiceAuthenticationExtensions`' own doc comment
    ("finalized/locked down in TASK-042"), not a gap this task introduces or should paper over.

Tests (all new, all against real dependencies rather than mocks):
  - `ServiceClientTokenProviderTests.cs` - caching/refresh in isolation via a fake `/token` handler
    and a `FakeTimeProvider` (added `Microsoft.Extensions.TimeProvider.Testing` to the test project):
    one mint call reused across repeated calls before expiry, a fresh mint once the cache is past its
    refresh margin.
  - `GatewayAdminProxyHost.cs` + `AdminProxyEndpointsTests.cs` - the real Gateway calling out, over
    real HTTP, to the real Ingestor and the real Processor, each guarded by the real Auth Service.
    Required aliasing THREE cross-service project references at once for the first time in this
    codebase (`AuthApi`/`IngestorApi`/`ProcessorApi`) - Ingestor's own top-level `Program`, despite
    having no explicit `public partial class Program;` marker anywhere in its source, still collided
    (CS0433) with this project's own `Program` once referenced unaliased, so all three needed it, not
    just the two with an explicit marker. Every downstream `HttpClient`'s primary handler is swapped
    for the target's own `TestServer.CreateHandler` (only the transport is in-memory - four in-process
    test servers have no real ports to reach each other on), the same technique
    `IngestorAdminHost`/`ProcessorAdminHost` already use for the JWKS client. Asserts the proxied
    response body is byte-for-byte identical to a direct call's, proving the acceptance criterion
    rather than a hand-written expected shape.
  - `ReadingsExportTests.cs` - 250 seeded readings one minute apart (large enough that a per-row
    streaming bug, not just an off-by-one on the first/last row, would show up), filtered by
    location/time-window and unfiltered cases.
  - `OpenApiTests.cs` - `/openapi/v1.json` and `/swagger/index.html` both serve successfully in
    Development; Swagger UI returns 404 outside it, mirroring the same Development-only gate
    `GraphQlHardeningTests` already proves for GraphQL introspection.

One real bug found and fixed during my own verification (not by a killed prior session this time,
since I wrote this from scratch): the CSV export test's own time-range filter only covered a 61-minute
window while asserting all 250 seeded readings should appear - the filter was working correctly
(61 was the right answer for that window), the test's window was just too narrow for the row count it
was seeding. Widened the window and moved the "must be excluded" reading further outside it so
widening the window didn't accidentally pull that reading back in-range too.

Verification:
  - `dotnet build WeakAppHandler.sln` - 0 warnings, 0 errors.
  - `dotnet test WeakAppHandler.sln` - 239/239 passed (32 in Gateway.Api.Tests alone, 10 new).

[2026-09-04] TASK-030 - DONE
Notification REST CRUD /api/v1/alert-rules with FluentValidation (valid operator, non-negative
cooldown, hysteresis in a reasonable range), admin-role-only access.
================================================================================

Picked per TASK-026's own NEXT note - the second of two tied-"medium" tasks whose completion
unblocks TASK-040 (priority "high"). Branch `task/task-030-alert-rules-crud` from up-to-date main.

Unusual session again: found the branch with only a package reference staged (FluentValidation added
to Directory.Packages.props and the Notification.Api csproj - a killed ralph.sh iteration got that
far and no further), then partway through implementing it from scratch discovered ANOTHER live
Claude Code session's background subagent independently writing the exact same feature into the same
files concurrently (confirmed by direct cross-session message exchange, same coordination pattern as
TASK-044). That peer killed its subagent once notified and stood down; I finished the implementation
and this session handled build/test/commit/PR/merge alone, as agreed.

What was built, reusing an already-in-place design foundation from a much earlier task:
`AlertRuleConfiguration`'s check constraints (`ck_alert_rules_cooldown_seconds`,
`ck_alert_rules_hysteresis_percent`, `ck_alert_rules_single_threshold`) already anticipated this
task's exact validation rules, so the request-level rules mirror database invariants that already
existed rather than inventing new ones.
  - `Admin/AlertRuleRequest.cs`/`AlertRuleResponse.cs` - wire DTOs. Operator/severity are the same
    lower-case codes `AlertOperatorConverter`/`AlertSeverityConverter` already use for the column
    itself. `HysteresisPercent`/`CooldownSeconds`/`IsEnabled` are nullable: omitted means "use the
    entity's own default" (5%/300s/enabled).
  - `Admin/AlertRuleRequestValidator.cs` (FluentValidation, invoked explicitly in the controller
    rather than through ASP.NET Core auto-validation - the author deprecated
    FluentValidation.AspNetCore, so there is no such integration package to add): valid operator/
    severity code, non-negative cooldown, hysteresis in [0, 100], exactly one threshold kind. The
    threshold check targets `RuleFor(r => r.ThresholdNumeric)` specifically (not the whole request
    object) so the resulting failure's `PropertyName` is actually "ThresholdNumeric" -
    `WithName`/`WithMessage` only change the rendered message, not what `ModelState` gets keyed by.
  - `Admin/AlertRulesController.cs` - full CRUD (`GET`/`GET {id}`/`POST`/`PUT {id}`/`DELETE {id}`)
    under `[Authorize(Policy = ServicePolicies.Admin)]` - a human admin's ROLE claim, not a machine
    scope, since PRD explicitly calls for role-gating a browser-facing surface here, unlike the
    Ingestor's/Processor's machine-only admin APIs. Failed validation becomes the same
    `ModelState.AddModelError` + `ValidationProblem(ModelState)` shape TASK-017's config PUT endpoint
    already established, so every admin REST surface in this codebase reports field-level 400s the
    same way.
  - `Program.cs` - registers `TimeProvider.System` (TryAdd, so a test can pin `CreatedAt`/`UpdatedAt`)
    and the validator; `AddControllers()`/auth were already wired from TASK-031.

Tests:
  - `AlertRuleRequestValidatorTests.cs` (new, pure unit) - every documented operator/severity code
    accepted, each invalid case failing on the specific field FluentValidation reports it under.
  - `AlertRulesAdminHost.cs` (new) - mirrors `AlertsHubHost` (TASK-031)'s real-Auth-Service
    arrangement, extended with a real admin token alongside the viewer one (the admin surface's
    negative case needs a token that authenticates but carries the wrong role).
  - `AlertRulesAdminEndpointsTests.cs` (new) - invalid operator/cooldown/hysteresis/threshold each
    return 400 with the right field name; a viewer token gets 403, no token gets 401; full CRUD
    against the real database, with counts scoped to the test's own metric code/seed rule ids rather
    than a raw table-wide row count - the database is shared across the whole collection, so other
    tests' own rules are present too and a total-count assertion would be flaky (found and fixed
    during my own verification: two tests initially asserted an exact total of 5, which failed with
    8 once other test classes' concurrently-seeded rules were accounted for).

[2026-09-04] TASK-040 - DONE
Frontend Administration screen (admin-role only): alert-rule CRUD with inline validation, and an
ingestion control panel (last poll outcome, failure counts by reason, circuit breaker state,
interval control, manual trigger button).
================================================================================

Picked as the highest-priority ready task: "high" priority, and its three dependencies
(TASK-035/TASK-030/TASK-026) were all done per TASK-030's own NEXT note. TASK-042 (critical) was
NOT eligible - it depends on TASK-041, which is still pending. Branch
`task/task-040-admin-screen` from up-to-date main.

Researched the actual state of the frontend/backend before writing anything (via a research
subagent, then direct file reads to confirm the parts that mattered for a design decision):
`AdministrationPage.tsx` was already a routed stub ("coming in TASK-040/TASK-041"). Auth/role
gating does NOT exist client-side yet (`accessTokenStore.ts` is `null` until TASK-041 lands a real
login flow) - so "admin only" in this task's title is descriptive intent, not something to enforce
here; TASK-041's own acceptance criteria are exactly "hide the Administration route for viewer" and
none of TASK-040's three test_steps require a logged-in session (step 1 is client-side validation
that blocks the network call entirely; steps 2/3 go through the Gateway's already-open admin proxy,
not through user auth). Building an auth gate now would be scope creep into TASK-041's job.

Two real gaps found in the existing backend that this task had to close, not just consume:
  - The Gateway's `AdminProxyController` (TASK-026) only proxied `GET status`/`GET stats` - no
    `POST /ingestion/trigger` or `PUT /ingestion/config`. Without those, the manual-trigger button
    and interval control would have nothing reachable from a browser (the Ingestor's own admin API
    requires a machine `ingestion:admin` JWT no browser session can obtain). Extended the controller
    with both, refactoring the existing `ProxyGetAsync` into a general `ProxySendAsync(clientName,
    method, path, content, ct)` - `PUT /config` forwards the incoming request body as a raw
    `StreamContent` rather than binding it to a Gateway-side DTO, keeping the same "proxied
    verbatim" design TASK-026 established for the GETs.
  - There is no GraphQL mutation type anywhere in `schema.graphql` (`Program.cs` registers only
    Query/Subscription) - alert-rule writes have no Gateway-proxied path at all, unlike ingestion.
    Reads go through the existing `alertRules` GraphQL query; create/update/delete go straight to
    Notification.Api's own REST CRUD (TASK-030) - the first place this frontend calls a REST
    endpoint that isn't the Gateway. Needed a new `VITE_NOTIFICATION_API_URL` env var alongside a
    new `VITE_GATEWAY_REST_URL` (same origin as the existing GraphQL URLs, just the REST proxy
    surface instead).

What was built:
  - `src/Gateway/.../Admin/AdminProxyController.cs` - `POST ingestion/trigger` and
    `PUT ingestion/config` added, `ProxySendAsync` generalization described above.
  - `tests/.../AdminProxyEndpointsTests.cs` - three new tests against the real
    `GatewayAdminProxyHost` (real Ingestor, real Auth Service, Testcontainers Postgres/RabbitMQ):
    trigger runs a real poll and returns its outcome; a valid config PUT changes the interval
    (verified by reading it back from `/status`); an invalid one (below the pipeline's
    `TotalTimeoutSeconds`) has its 400 forwarded through the proxy unchanged.
  - `frontend/src/shared/rest/restClient.ts` (new) - the frontend's first REST client wrapper.
    Attaches `Authorization: Bearer` from `accessTokenStore` the same way `apolloClient.ts`'s
    authLink does (read fresh per call, currently always absent until TASK-041); throws `RestError`
    (status + parsed `ValidationProblemDetails.errors`) on any non-2xx response.
  - `frontend/src/features/administration/ingestion/` - `ingestionApi.ts` (typed wrappers over the
    three Gateway-proxied endpoints), `useIngestionAdmin.ts` (status fetch + trigger/config actions,
    each re-fetching status afterward so the panel reflects the Ingestor's real state rather than a
    locally-guessed one), `IngestionPanel.tsx` (pure presentation - facts list, circuit-breaker badge
    using a neutral token rather than the reserved severity scale, failure-by-reason list, trigger
    button, interval form).
  - `frontend/src/features/administration/alert-rules/` - `alertRuleValidation.ts` (client-side
    mirror of `AlertRuleRequestValidator`'s exact rules: operator/severity enums, non-negative
    cooldown, hysteresis in [0,100], exactly one threshold kind - this is what makes TASK-040's own
    acceptance criterion true: an invalid submission never reaches the network), `alertRulesApi.ts`
    (REST CRUD against Notification.Api), `alertRuleMapping.ts` (GraphQL read-model row -> form
    values; the GraphQL schema's uppercase enums vs the REST wire's lower-case codes are identical
    strings modulo case, so `.toLowerCase()` is the whole mapping), `useAlertRulesAdmin.ts` (GraphQL
    `useQuery` for reads + REST calls for writes, each followed by a `refetch()` of the GraphQL list
    rather than hand-rolling optimistic cache updates), `AlertRuleForm.tsx` (create/edit form -
    metric selection auto-derives numeric-vs-boolean threshold input from
    `entities/meter/metricDisplay.ts`'s existing kind mapping; errors computed every render but only
    shown after a first submit attempt, so an empty create form doesn't open pre-flagged),
    `AlertRulesTable.tsx`, `AlertRulesAdminContent.tsx` (owns which rule is being created/edited;
    table and form themselves stay pure).
  - `frontend/src/app/pages/AdministrationPage.tsx` - rewritten from the TASK-040/041 stub into two
    `AsyncBoundary`-wrapped sections, following the same loading-skeleton/error-retry shape every
    other screen already uses.
  - `frontend/src/shared/config/runtimeConfig.ts` / `.env.example` - added `gatewayRestUrl` and
    `notificationApiUrl`.

One real ESLint failure worth recording for next time: `eslint-plugin-react-hooks` 7's new
`react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` rule flags a plain "fetch on mount" `useEffect(() => { void
refetch() }, [refetch])` when `refetch` is an async function whose first statement is
`setLoading(true)` - that call runs *synchronously* the moment `refetch()` is invoked (an async
function body runs synchronously up to its first `await`), so from the effect's perspective it's
indistinguishable from calling `setState` directly in the effect body. Fixed by having the mount
effect call the fetch function directly and only touch state inside its `.then` callbacks (a
microtask, genuinely after the effect returns) rather than delegating to the same `refetch` used by
retry buttons. Same rule flagged a `useEffect` that resynced local input state from a prop
(`IngestionPanel`'s interval field) - fixed using React's documented "adjusting state when a prop
changes" pattern (setState conditionally during render, comparing against a mirrored previous-value
state) instead of a `useEffect`, which is what react.dev itself recommends over an effect for this
exact case.

Also hit `erasableSyntaxOnly` (TS 6 in this scaffold) rejecting TypeScript parameter-property
shorthand (`constructor(public readonly status: number, ...)`) in the new `RestError` class - not
just real `enum`, as TASK-033's original comment about this flag only mentioned. Rewrote as
explicit field declarations + assignment in the constructor body.

Did not attempt a live browser check: PowerShell command execution in this session is blocked by
its own permission sandbox even for a bare `Get-Command` lookup (returns "contains multiple
operations" / requires approval that a non-interactive session can't grant), so a dev server could
not be started and driven here. Verified instead via `dotnet build`/`dotnet test` (backend) and
`npm run build`/`npm run lint`/`npm test` (frontend) - see below.

Verification:
  - `dotnet build WeakAppHandler.sln` - 0 warnings, 0 errors.
  - `dotnet test gateway.slnf` - 35/35 passed (includes the 3 new admin-proxy tests, run against
    real Testcontainers Postgres/RabbitMQ and a real in-process Auth Service).
  - `npm run build` (frontend) - `tsc -b && vite build` succeeds.
  - `npm run lint` (frontend) - clean.
  - `npm test` (frontend) - 135/135 passed across 26 files (12 new: alertRuleValidation,
    AlertRuleForm, IngestionPanel, plus the existing suite unaffected).

NEXT: TASK-041 (frontend auth: login form, in-memory access token, hides Administration for
viewer) is now the natural next step - it depends on TASK-040 (done) and TASK-009 (done, backend

[2026-09-04] TASK-041 - DONE
Frontend authentication: login form, in-memory access token (never localStorage/sessionStorage),
transparent refresh via the httpOnly refresh cookie, Administration hidden from nav and blocked on
direct URL navigation for the viewer role.
================================================================================

Picked as the natural next task per TASK-040's own NEXT note. Branch `task/task-041-frontend-auth`
from up-to-date main.

Found the branch already substantially implemented by a killed ralph.sh iteration and reviewed it
in full before running anything:
  - `shared/auth/accessTokenStore.ts` - the ONLY place the access token lives (a module-level
    variable, never persisted), with a subscribe/notify seam so the REST client, both realtime
    channels, and the UI (nav gating, header status) can all read the current session without a
    React context threading it through every layer.
  - `shared/auth/authApi.ts` - calls the Auth Service directly with `credentials: 'include'` for
    `/login`/`/refresh` (not through the Gateway's REST proxy, and not through
    `shared/rest/restClient.ts`, which unconditionally attaches a bearer token neither call has yet).
  - `shared/auth/authSessionManager.ts` - schedules a silent refresh 30s ahead of the access token's
    real expiry (`REFRESH_SKEW_MS`), so a request straddling the boundary never carries an
    already-expired token; a failed refresh (revoked/expired cookie, Auth Service unreachable) just
    ends the session rather than erroring, since the next protected action naturally redirects to
    /login. `resumeAuthSession` (called once at app startup) is what survives a full page reload
    despite the access token being deliberately memory-only - the reload wipes the module-level
    variable, but the 7-day httpOnly refresh cookie does not.
  - `app/RequireAdmin.tsx` - redirects home rather than a bespoke access-denied screen; `nav-items.ts`
    gained an `adminOnly` flag AppShell filters on, so a viewer never sees the Administration link at
    all, and typing the URL directly still hits the route guard.
  - `shared/auth/AuthStatus.tsx` - header widget: a "Log in" link when signed out, identity + a
    logout control when signed in. `logout()` only clears the in-memory session (the Auth Service has
    no revoke endpoint - out of this task's frontend-only scope; a returning visit within the
    cookie's 7-day lifetime resumes transparently again).

One real bug found and fixed during my own verification, not by the killed session (its own
progress.txt entry, if it wrote one, never ran the suite to find this): `AppShell.test.tsx`'s
"shows a Log in link... signed-in identity..." test called `setAuthSession(...)` directly in the
test body, outside of `act()`, then asserted on the DOM immediately afterward. `setAuthSession`'s
listener notification calls a React state setter (`useAuthSession`'s `useState` setter) completely
outside any render or event-handler context React's testing utilities know to wrap, so the
resulting re-render was not guaranteed to have flushed before the assertion ran (and did not, in
the actual run - `getByText('admin@example.com · Admin')` failed to find the element). Fixed by
wrapping the `setAuthSession` call in `act(() => ...)`, forcing the update to flush synchronously
first.

Tests: `accessTokenStore.test.ts`, `authSessionManager.test.ts` (fake timers driving the actual
30s-before-expiry refresh schedule and its failure path), `RequireAdmin.test.tsx`,
`loginValidation.test.ts`/`LoginForm.test.tsx`, plus `AppShell.test.tsx` extended for nav-hiding and
the header identity widget - 160/160 passed after the one fix above.

Verification:
  - `npm run build` (via `prebuild`'s codegen regeneration) - 686 modules transformed, 0 TypeScript
    errors.
  - `npm run lint` - clean.
  - `npm test -- --run` - 160/160 passed (31 files) after the `act()` fix; failed 1/160 before it.

[2026-09-04] TASK-007 - DONE
Architecture documentation: docs/architecture.md with a service topology diagram and communication
rules; docs/adr/ with ADRs for the four required decisions.
================================================================================

Picked deliberately out of priority order: TASK-042 (the actual next "critical" pick per TASK-041's
own NEXT note) was already being worked by another live Claude Code session in the same shared
working directory when this session reached it (confirmed by direct cross-session message exchange -
they had a subagent mid-flight rewriting ServiceAuthenticationExtensions/ServiceCorsExtensions/every
service's Program.cs/the whole Gateway.Api.Tests auth wiring). Rather than idle waiting for their
merge or risk clobbering deep in-progress work on a shared branch, picked TASK-007 instead - a
verified zero-file-overlap task (pure new markdown under docs/, nothing under src/ or tests/) - and
did the work in an isolated `git worktree` (`../WeakAppHandler-task007`, its own checkout on its own
branch) rather than switching the shared working directory's branch, since switching branches there
while the other session's subagent might still be writing to disk could have corrupted or lost their
uncommitted work.

Wrote `docs/architecture.md` and four ADRs, cross-checking every factual claim against the actual
source tree rather than the PRD's original text (which predates several real implementation
decisions and, per this document's own "Implementation notes" section, get a few technology choices
wrong - no MediatR, no Recharts, no FluentAssertions, no Playwright anywhere in the solution, despite
all four appearing in PRD §5's table):
  - `docs/adr/0001-ingestor-processor-responsibility-split.md` - the Ingestor has no database
    connection at all; every poll attempt publishes `IngestAttemptRecorded` (exact record shape
    quoted from `src/Contracts/WeakAppHandler.Contracts/IngestAttemptRecorded.cs`), which is the
    only way `ingest_batches` ever gets written, from the Processor's own consumer.
  - `docs/adr/0002-independent-migration-ownership.md` - three independent migrating `DbContext`s
    (Auth/Processor/Notification, one per owned table set) plus the Gateway's two read-only,
    non-migrating ones; documents the real TASK-032 bug (`ApplyConfigurationsFromAssembly` letting
    two contexts in the same assembly absorb each other's entity configurations) as a concrete
    consequence of why the read-only contexts use explicit per-entity `ApplyConfiguration` instead.
  - `docs/adr/0003-no-gateway-domain-layer.md` - verified no `WeakAppHandler.Gateway.Domain` project
    exists and no GraphQL `Mutation` type exists in the schema; every Gateway write is either
    proxied verbatim (admin REST) or not accepted at all (alert-rule CRUD goes straight from the
    frontend to Notification's own REST API, bypassing the Gateway).
  - `docs/adr/0004-signalr-single-replica-limitation.md` - both `AddSignalR()` (Notification) and
    `AddInMemorySubscriptions()` (Gateway) confirmed to have no backplane configured; documents this
    as symmetric and additive-to-fix (swap the extension method, no consumer restructuring needed)
    rather than a structural flaw.

No build/test suite applies to markdown-only documentation; verification here was reading every
factual claim back against the real source files it describes (entity names, method names, exact
message contracts, project structure) rather than trusting memory of earlier sessions' work.

Second consecutive pick made specifically because TASK-042 (the actual next "critical" priority)
remained claimed by the other session throughout this task too. Done in its own isolated `git
worktree` (`../WeakAppHandler-task045`) for the same reason as TASK-007: the shared working

Verified every Prometheus metric name and label this dashboard's panels query against the REAL
OpenTelemetry Prometheus exporter output rather than guessing at OTel's naming/unit-suffix
conventions - wrote a disposable ASP.NET Core probe referencing the real `WeakAppHandler.ServiceDefaults`
project (so it gets the exact same `ConfigureServiceTelemetry` wiring every real service does),
registered `Meter`/`Counter`/`Histogram`/`ObservableGauge` instruments matching each service's real
instrument names (`IngestorMetrics`, `ProcessorMetrics`, `NotificationMetrics`, `GatewayMetrics`,
MassTransit's own built-in instrumentation), and read the actual `/metrics` scrape output. This is
what caught, before writing a single dashboard panel, that OTel's Prometheus exporter expands unit
abbreviations into full words (`ms` -> `..._milliseconds_bucket/_sum/_count`) and drops
count-style bracketed units (`{poll}`/`{message}`/`{state}`) entirely from the metric name, and that
every tag key with a dot in it (`messaging.masstransit.destination`) becomes underscore-separated
in the exposed label name - none of which is obvious from the C# source alone.

What was built:
  - `deploy/prometheus/prometheus.yml` - scrape config targeting `ingestor`/`processor`/`gateway`/
    `notification` on port 8080 (matching the in-container port convention `weak-app`'s own compose
    entry already uses) - the service names TASK-047's docker-compose.yml will assign once it
    containerises the application services, which have no Dockerfiles yet as of this task.
  - `deploy/grafana/provisioning/datasources/prometheus.yml` - auto-provisions the one Prometheus
    data source, pointed at the `prometheus` service TASK-047 will place on the same `observability`
    network Grafana sits on.
  - `deploy/grafana/provisioning/dashboards/dashboards.yml` - the dashboard *provider* definition
    (lives in `/etc/grafana/provisioning/dashboards`), pointed at a *separate* directory
    (`/var/lib/grafana/dashboards`) for the actual dashboard JSON, so Grafana never tries to parse
    this YAML file itself as a dashboard.
  - `deploy/grafana/dashboards/weakapphandler-overview.json` - one dashboard, eight panels covering
    every F10 metric: poll outcomes (the WeakApp-failure-rate panel the acceptance criteria calls
    out by name), the circuit-breaker state gauge (the other acceptance-criteria panel, with a value
    mapping from 0-3 to Closed/HalfOpen/Open/Isolated), poll latency p95, queue consumption rate
    (MassTransit's own receive-rate metric), processing latency p95, deduplicated/dead-lettered
    counts, alerts raised/resolved by severity, and GraphQL request duration p95.

Verification, since the real docker-compose.yml has no Prometheus/Grafana containers yet
(TASK-047's job) and this task's own acceptance criteria assume `docker compose up` already brings
them up: reproduced the same provisioning mechanism directly with real containers rather than
trusting the YAML/JSON was correct by inspection alone.
  - Ran the disposable metrics probe above, confirming its real `/metrics` output byte-for-byte
    matches every metric name and label this dashboard's panel queries reference.
  - Started a real `prom/prometheus` container with a scrape config pointed at the probe
    (`host.docker.internal`) and confirmed via Prometheus's own `/api/v1/targets` that the scrape
    target came up healthy.
  - Started a real `grafana/grafana` container with `deploy/grafana/provisioning` and
    `deploy/grafana/dashboards` bind-mounted exactly as TASK-047's compose file will need to mount
    them, and confirmed via Grafana's own API (`/api/datasources`, `/api/search?type=dash-db`) that
    both the data source and the dashboard provisioned automatically with zero manual steps - the
    literal acceptance criterion.
  - Ran every one of the dashboard's eight PromQL queries through Grafana's own datasource-proxy
    endpoint and confirmed each resolves with the correct label set and, for the two counters set to
    a known non-zero value in the probe (`processor_messages_deduplicated_total` = 2,
    `processor_messages_dead_lettered_total` = 1), the exact expected value round-tripped through
    Prometheus and Grafana correctly. The two `rate()`-based queries returned 0 and one
    `histogram_quantile` returned `NaN` - both expected and understood, not bugs: a synthetic
    counter set once has no meaningful rate yet, and a histogram quantile needs more than one scrape
    sample in its window to resolve to a real number, which a freshly-started monitoring stack
    (real or this verification) does not have in its first few seconds either.
  - One real environment mistake found and fixed mid-verification, not a code bug: Git Bash's
    automatic MSYS path conversion silently rewrote container-side bind-mount paths like
    `/etc/grafana/provisioning` into a Windows path before `docker run` ever saw them, which is why
    the first verification attempt showed nothing provisioned at all despite the files being
    correct - `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` on the `docker run`/`docker exec` calls fixed it.
  - Tore down every verification container/network afterward; nothing here is part of the commit.
